He will be publicly hauled over the coals for lack of energy, and unless his labours fructify he is liable to be lowered to an inferior post. Thus it is in the interest of Communist officials to coax, cajole, or even compel soldiers to enter the ranks of the party. The statistics supplied are collected at headquarters and summaries are published. It is according to these statistics that the membership of the Communist Party is a little more than half a million, out of the 120 or 130 million inhabitants of Soviet Russia.
---
Another feature of the Red army which is worthy of note is the group of organizations known as “Cultural-Enlightenment Committees,” which are entrusted with the work of entertaining and “enlightening” the soldiers. Being partly of an educational character the collaboration of non-Communists on these committees is indispensable, though rigid Communist control renders free participation by intellectuals impossible. There is also a lack of books. A department at headquarters, in which Maxim Gorky is interested, deals with the publication of scientific and literary works, but compared with the deluge of propagandist literature the work of his department is nil. The cultural-enlightenment committees arrange lectures on scientific subjects, dramatic performances, concerts, and cinema shows. The entertainments consist chiefly of the staging of “proletarian” plays, written to the order of the department of propaganda. From the artistic standpoint these plays are exceedingly bad﻿—unmitigated Bolshevist atrocities﻿—but their strong point is that they represent the class-struggle in a vivid and lurid light. As no one would go to see them alone, other plays, usually farces, or musical items are thrown in by way of attraction. Propagandist speeches by Lenin, Trotsky, Zinoviev and others, reproduced on gramophones, are sometimes reeled off in the intervals. Schools of reading and writing are attached to some cultural-enlightenment committees.
In my regiment we had no cultural-enlightenment committee.

===Chinese===

不言而喻，由于怕受申斥，这类报告一律拣最光鲜的写。军队里尤其如此。一个"细胞"的人数若不见涨，主管的政委或政治指导员就要被追问缘由，公开地拉出来烤打一顿，训斥其不卖力气；劳绩再不开花，就等着降职。所以，哄着、诓着、甚至押着士兵入党，成了共产党官吏的利益所在。呈报的统计在总部汇总公布。据这些统计，共产党党员五十万挂零——苏维埃俄国的人口是一亿二三千万。

---

红军还有一个值得注意的特色，就是那批称作"文化启蒙委员会"的组织，专管娱乐士兵并给他们"启蒙"。因为带几分教育的性质，非共产党人的协作在委员会里不可缺；只是共产党的严苛控制，使知识分子无从自由参与。书也缺。总部有一个高尔基参与的部门，专管科学与文学著作的出版，可是比起宣传品的大洪水，它那点产出等于零。文化启蒙委员会安排科普讲演、话剧、音乐会和电影。娱乐节目主要是上演"无产阶级"戏剧，都按宣传部的订单写就。从艺术上看，这些戏坏透了——不折不扣的布尔什维克暴行——其长处是把阶级斗争写得又生动又血腥。因为没人肯单为看它们而来，总得搭上别的戏，通常是笑剧，或者加点音乐节目凑趣。列宁、托洛茨基、季诺维也夫等人演说的留声机唱片，有时在幕间一放到底。有的文化启蒙委员会还附设识字班。

我团没有文化启蒙委员会。

===Original===

Being unnecessary for purposes of control they were not so universal as the “cells,” but depended to some extent for their establishment upon the enterprise of the commissar. Living, however, mostly in Petrograd, I came in touch through friends with other regiments than my own, and attended entertainments got up by cultural-enlightenment committees until I knew the propagandist speeches, which were always the same, almost by heart. Let me describe one such meeting. It was in the regiment of which Morozov was commissar. At this particular meeting I was to have functioned as amateur accompanist and should have done so if one of the singers, from a Petrograd theatre, had not unexpectedly brought a professional with her.6
The organizer of this entertainment, though he played but little part in the performance, deserves a word of mention. As a sailor, of about twenty years of age, he differed greatly from his fellows. He was not ill-favoured in looks, unintelligent but upright, and occupied the post of chairman of the Poor Committee of a house where I was an habitual visitor. I will refer to him as Comrade Rykov. Like Morozov, Rykov had had only an elementary education and knew nothing of history, geography, or literature. History for him dated from Karl Marx, whom he was taught to regard rather as the Israelites did Moses; while his conception of geography was confined to a division of the world’s surface into Red and un-Red. Soviet Russia was Red, capitalistic countries (of which he believed there were very few) were White; and “White” was an adjective no less odious than “bourgeois.” But Rykov’s instincts were none the less good and it was with a genuine desire to better the lot of the proletariat that he had drifted into “the party.” Under the Tsarist regime he had suffered maltreatment. He had seen his comrades bullied and oppressed. The first months of the revolution had been too tempestuous, especially for the sailors, and the forces at issue too complex, for a man of Rykov’s stamp to comprehend the causes underlying the failure of the Provisional Government. To him the Soviet Government personified the Revolution itself.

===Chinese===

既然控制用不着它，它便不像"细胞"那样无孔不入，设不设，多少要看政委的干劲。不过我多半住在彼得格勒，借朋友们的引线接触到了别的团队，文化启蒙委员会办的娱乐会也参加到几乎能把那千篇一律的宣传讲演背下来的地步。且容我描述其中一场。会是在莫罗佐夫当政委的那个团开的。那一场我本要客串业余伴奏，若不是彼得格勒剧院来的那位歌手出其不意自带了一位专业的。6

这台晚会的组织者虽在演出里戏份寥寥，也值得一记。他是个水兵，二十岁上下，同他的弟兄们大不一样。相貌不恶，谈不上聪明但为人正直，兼任着一所我常去的房子的贫民委员会主席。姑且称他雷科夫同志。同莫罗佐夫一样，雷科夫只受过初等教育，历史、地理、文学一概不知。历史，在他从卡尔·马克思起算——他被教得看待马克思，同以色列人看待摩西相仿；地理，在他就是把世界分成红的与非红的。苏维埃俄国是红的；资本主义国家（他相信为数寥寥）是白的；而"白"这个形容词，同"资产阶级"一样可憎。但雷科夫的天性终究不坏，他是揣着一颗真想改善无产阶级命运的心，漂进"党"里来的。沙皇治下他受过虐待，眼见同伴们挨欺受压。革命头几个月风浪太大——对水兵尤其如此——争斗的力量又太错综，雷科夫这般资质的人无从理解临时政府失败的原因。在他，苏维埃政府就是革命本身的化身。

===Original===

A few catchphrases, such as “dictatorship of the proletariat,” “tyranny of the bourgeoisie,” “robber-capitalism,” “Soviet emancipation” completely dominated his mind and it seemed to him indisputably just that the definition of these terms should be left absolutely to the great ones who had conceived them. Thus Rykov, like most Communists, was utterly blind to inconsistencies. The discussion of policy, especially of foreign policy, of which the rest of the world hears so much, was not attempted by him. Rykov accepted his directions unhesitatingly from “those who knew.” He never asked himself why the party was so small, and popular discontent he attributed, as he was told to do, to the pernicious agitation of Mensheviks and Socialist-Revolutionaries, who were but Monarchists in disguise. Rykov was the type of man the Bolsheviks were striving their utmost to entice into the Communist Party. He had three supreme recommendations: he was a untiring worker, his genuinely good motives would serve to popularize the party, and he never thought. It is independent thinkers the Bolsheviks cannot tolerate. Rykov, like a good Communist, accepted the dogma propounded from above and that was the alpha and omega of his creed. But when it came to doing something to improve the lot of his fellows, and, incidentally, to lead them into the true Communist path, Rykov was all there. In other realms he would have made an ideal Y.M.C.A. or Salvation Army worker, and it was not surprising that he was in great demand whenever it was a question of amusing or entertaining the soldiers.
The hall was decorated with red flags. Portraits of Lenin, Trotsky, Zinoviev, and of course of Karl Marx, wreathed in red bunting and laurels, decorated the walls. Over the stage hung a crude inscription painted on cardboard: “Long live the Soviet Power,” while similar inscriptions, “Proletarians of all countries, unite,” and “Long live the World Revolution,” were hung around. The audience, consisting of the regiment and numerous guests, sat on wooden forms and disregarded the injunction not to smoke.
The entertainment began with the singing of the “Internationale,” the hymn of the World Revolution. The music of this song is as un-Russian, unmelodious, banal, and uninspiring as any music could possibly be.

===Chinese===

"无产阶级专政""资产阶级暴政""强盗资本主义""苏维埃的解放"——几句口头禅完全占据了他的头脑；至于这些名词的定义该由构想它们的伟人们一锤定音，在他看来更是天经地义。于是雷科夫同多数共产党人一样，对种种自相矛盾全然盲目。政策的讨论，尤其是外界听得很多的对外政策，他想也不去想。雷科夫毫不犹豫地听命于"懂行的人"。他从不问自己党为什么这样小；民间的不满，他照吩咐归罪于孟什维克和社会革命党人的恶毒煽动——那不过是化了装的君主派。雷科夫正是布尔什维克拼命要诱入共产党的那类人。他有三张顶好的荐书：不知疲倦地干活，动机纯正可为党增光，以及从不思考。布尔什维克容不下的，正是独立思考的人。雷科夫照一个好共产党人的本分，全盘领受上面颁布的教条，那就是他信条的阿尔法与俄梅戛。可一旦要为弟兄们做点实事，顺带引他们走上真正的共产党正道，雷科夫便样样在行。换个天地，他会是个理想的基督教青年会或救世军干事；每逢逗乐娱乐士兵的差事，他供不应求，也就不足为怪了。

会场挂着红旗。列宁、托洛茨基、季诺维也夫，当然还有卡尔·马克思的画像，缠着红绸与桂冠，装点四壁。台口悬着一块纸板画的粗陋标语："苏维埃政权万岁"；"全世界无产者，联合起来""世界革命万岁"之类的同样标语挂了一圈。观众是全团官兵加众多来宾，坐在长木凳上，全不理会"请勿吸烟"的告诫。

晚会以《国际歌》——世界革命的颂歌——齐唱开场。这支歌的音乐，同任何音乐能够的一样：不带俄国味，不动听，平庸，激不起人心。

===Original===

To listen to its never-ending repetition on every possible and impossible occasion is not the least of the inflictions which the Russian people are compelled to suffer under the present dispensation. When one compares it with the noble strains of the former national anthem, or with the revolutionary requiem which the Bolsheviks have happily not supplanted by any atrocity such as the “Internationale” but have inherited from their predecessors, or with national songs such as “Ey, Ukhnem,” or for that matter with any Russian folk-music, then the “Internationale” calls up a picture of some abominable weed protruding from the midst of a garden of beautiful and fragrant flowers.
The “Internationale” was sung with energy by those in the audience who knew the words, and the accompanist made up with bombastic pianistic flourishes for the silence of those who did not.
Nothing could have afforded a more remarkable contrast than the item that followed. It was an unaccompanied quartet by four soldiers who sang a number of Russian folk-songs and one or two composed by the leader of the four. If you have not listened to the Russian peasants of a summer evening singing to accompany their dances on the village green, you cannot know exactly what it meant to these peasant soldiers, cooped in their city barracks, to hear their songs re-sung. The singers had rehearsed carefully, the execution was excellent, the enthusiasm they aroused was unbounded, and they were recalled again and again. They would probably have gone on endlessly had not the Jewish agitator, who was acting as master of ceremonies and who had to make a speech later, announced that they must get along with the programme. The contrast between Bolshevism and Russianism could never have been more strikingly illustrated than by this accident of the “Internationale” being followed by Russian folk-songs. The former was an interpretation in sound of all the drab, monotonous unloveliness of the supposedly proletarian regime, the latter an interpretation in music of the unuttered yearnings of the Russian soul, aspiring after things unearthly, things beautiful, things spiritual.
There followed a selection of songs and romances by a lady singer from one of the musical-comedy theatres, and then the agitator rose. The job of a professional agitator is a coveted one in Red Russia.

===Chinese===

每逢可能与不可能的场合都要无休止地听它重奏，是俄国人民在眼下这套统治下不得不受的折磨里决不最轻的一项。把它同旧国歌的庄严乐句相比，同布尔什维克幸而未曾用《国际歌》这类暴行顶替、而是从前人承袭的那支革命安魂曲相比，同《伏尔加船夫曲》一类民歌乃至任何俄国民族音乐相比，《国际歌》便像一座姹紫嫣红、芬芳馥郁的花园正中，戳出来的一株可憎的毒草。

《国际歌》由会唱的听众奋力唱了，不会唱的沉默部分，伴奏者便用浮夸的钢琴花腔补足。

紧随其后的一个节目，对比之鲜明无以复加。那是四个士兵的无伴奏四重唱，唱了好几首俄国民歌，还有一两首出自四人中领头的自创。你若没有听过俄国农民夏晚在村中草场上边舞边歌，便无法确切知道，这些困在城里营房的农家子弟再听到自己的歌，意味着什么。歌者们精心排练过，唱得出色，激起的热情没有边际，一而再、再而三地被请出来。若不是司仪——那个还要发表演说的犹太鼓动家——宣布节目得往下走，他们大概能一直唱下去。布尔什维主义同俄罗斯精神的对比，再没有比这桩巧合更醒目的了：《国际歌》之后，接的是俄国民歌。前者是用声音对那套号称无产阶级政权的灰暗、单调、无可爱处的全部诠释；后者是用音乐对俄罗斯灵魂未说出口的渴望的诠释——向往超脱尘世之物、美好之物、精神之物。

接着是一位轻歌剧剧院的女歌手唱了几支歌和浪漫曲，然后鼓动家登台了。职业鼓动家，在红色俄国是份美差。

===Original===

A good agitator is regarded as a very important functionary, and receives high pay. Coached in his arguments and phraseology in the propagandist schools of the capitals, he has nothing whatever to do but talk as loudly and as frequently as possible, merely embellishing his speeches in such a way as to make them forceful and, if possible, attractive. He requires no logic, and consequently no brains, for he is guaranteed against heckling by the Bolshevist system of denouncing political opponents as “enemies of the State” and imprisoning them. Thus the entire stock-in-trade of a professional agitator consists of “words, words, words,” and the more he has of them the better for him.
The youth who mounted the stage and prepared to harangue the audience was nineteen years of age, of criminal past (at that very time he was charged by the Bolsheviks themselves with theft), and possessed of pronounced Hebrew features. His complexion was lustrous, his nose was aquiline and crooked, his mouth was small, and his eyes resembled those of a mouse. His discourse consisted of the usual exhortations to fight the landlord Whites. He was violent in his denunciation of the Allies, and of all non-Bolshevist Socialists. His speech closed somewhat as follows:

> So, comrades, you see that if we give in to the Whites all your land will go back to the landowners, all the factories to the moneymakers, and you will be crushed again under the yoke of the murderous bankers, priests, generals, landlords, police, and other hirelings of bourgeois tyranny. They will whip you into slavery, and they will ride to wealth on the bleeding backs of you, your wives, and your children. Only we Communists can save you from the bloody rage of the White demons. Let us defend Red Petrograd to the last drop of our blood! Down with the English and French imperialist bloodsuckers! Long live the proletarian World Revolution!

===Chinese===

一个好的鼓动家被看作要员，薪水优厚。他先在两京的宣传学校里把论点和词句操演纯熟，此后便只管扯开嗓子、多多益善地讲，只需把演说修饰得有力，可能的话再动听些。他用不着逻辑，因而也用不着脑子——布尔什维克把政敌一律打成"人民公敌"投进监狱，保他没人喝倒彩。于是职业鼓动家的全部本钱就是"词儿，词儿，词儿"，词儿越多，于他越好。

登台准备训话的那个青年十九岁，有犯罪的前科（就在当时，他还被布尔什维克自己指控犯有偷窃），生着显著的希伯来人相貌。皮肤油亮，鹰钩鼻还带点弯，嘴小，眼睛像耗子。他的讲演不出那一套：号召同地主白军作战。他猛烈抨击协约国和一切非布尔什维克的社会主义者。演讲是这样收尾的：

> 所以，同志们，你们看清了：我们若是向白军低头，你们的土地全部回到地主手里，工厂全部回到财主手里，你们又要被压在杀人不眨眼的银行家、神甫、将军、地主、警察以及资产阶级暴政的别样走狗的轭下。他们要用鞭子把你们抽回奴隶身份，要骑着你们、你们老婆孩子的血背奔富贵。只有我们共产党人能救你们逃出白色恶魔的血怒。让我们保卫红色彼得格勒到最后一滴血！打倒英法帝国主义吸血鬼！无产阶级世界革命万岁！

===Original===

Having ended his speech, he signalled to the accompanist to strike up the “Internationale.” Then followed another strange contrast, one of those peculiar phenomena often met with in Russia, even in the Communist Party. A modest, nervous, and gentle-looking individual whom I did not know, as different from the previous speaker as water from fire, made a strangely earnest speech, urging the necessity of self-education as the only means of restoring Russia’s fallen fortunes. At the admission of fallen fortunes the Jew looked up with displeasure. He had sung the glories of the Red administration and the exploits of the Red army. It was not enough, said the speaker, that Russia had won the treasured Soviet Power﻿—that, of course, was an inestimable boon﻿—but until the people dragged themselves out of the morass of ignorance they could not profit by its benefits. The masses of Russia, he urged, should set strenuously to work to raise themselves culturally and spiritually, in order to fit themselves for the great task they were called upon to perform, namely, to effect the emancipation of the workers of all the world.
The “Internationale” was not sung when he concluded. There was too much sincerity in his speech, and the bombastic strains of that tune would have been sadly out of place. The rest of the programme consisted of two stage performances, enacted by amateurs, the first one a light comedy, and the second a series of propagandist tableaux, depicting the sudden emancipation of the worker by the Soviet Power, heralded by an angel dressed all in red. In one of these Comrade Rykov proudly participated. In the concluding tableau the Red angel was seen guarding a smiling workman and his family on one side, and a smiling peasant and family on the other, while the audience was invited to rise and sing the “Internationale.”
Of conscious political intelligence in the cultural-enlightenment committees there is none, nor under “iron party discipline” can there possibly be any. All Communist agitators repeat, parrot-like, the epithets and catchphrases dictated from above. None the less, despite their crudity and one-sidedness, these committees serve a positive purpose in the Red army. By the provision of entertainment the savagery of the soldiery has been curbed and literacy promoted.

===Chinese===

讲完，他向伴奏者示意奏《国际歌》。随后又是一重奇特的对比——俄国常见、连共产党内也常见的那种怪现象之一。一个我不认识的、谦卑拘谨、面目温良的人，同前一位演讲人水火之别，作了一场出奇恳切的演讲，力言自我教育乃是重振俄国衰落国运的唯一途径。听到"衰落的国运"，那犹太人怏怏地抬起了头。他方才还颂扬红色政权的光荣、红军的武功。演讲人说，俄国赢得了宝贵的苏维埃政权固然不错——那自是无价之恩——可人民若不把自己从愚昧的泥沼里拖出来，便享受不到它的好处。他吁请俄国的民众发奋在文化上、精神上提高自己，好担当那桩奉召去完成的大业：解放全世界的劳动者。

他讲完，没有唱《国际歌》。他话里的诚意太重，那支歌浮夸的调子响起来就太煞风景了。余下的节目是两出业余演出的戏：头一出轻喜剧，第二出是一组宣传活报，演的是工人被苏维埃政权一举解放，前导是一位通体红装的报喜天使。其中一幕，雷科夫同志还自豪地登了场。最后一幕里，只见红色天使一边护着一个含笑的工人和他的一家，一边护着一个含笑的农民和他的一家；观众则被请起立，齐唱《国际歌》。

文化启蒙委员会里，谈不上有什么自觉的政治头脑，在"铁的党纪"之下也谈不上可能有。共产党鼓动家个个鹦鹉学舌，重复上面颁发的谥号与口号。话虽如此，这些委员会粗陋归粗陋、片面归片面，在红军里却起着正面的作用：娱乐的供给，收敛了兵痞的凶野，也推广了识字。

===Original===

If they were nonpolitical and run by intelligent people with the sole object of improving the minds of the masses they might be made a real instrument for the furtherance of education and culture. At present they are often grotesque. But representing an “upward” trend, the cultural-enlightenment committees form a welcome contrast to the majority of Bolshevist institutions.
---
Our survey of the essential features of the Red army is now complete and may be summed up as follows:
1. A military machine, with all the attributes of other armies but differing in terminology. Its strength at the close of 1920 was said to be about two million, but this is probably exaggerated.
2. A concomitant organization, about one-tenth in size, of the Communist Party, permeating the entire army, subjected to military experts in purely military decisions, but with absolute powers of political and administrative control, supplemented by Special Departments of the Extraordinary Commission, Revolutionary Tribunals, and Special Commissions for Combating Desertion.
3. A network of Communist-controlled propagandist organizations called Cultural-Enlightenment Committees, whose object is the entertainment and education of the soldiers.
Tractable, docile, and leaderless though the Russian people are, the machine which has been built up in the Red army is still a monument to the inflexible will and merciless determination of its leader, Trotsky. Its development has been rapid and is perhaps not yet complete. Trotsky would make of it an absolutely soulless, will-less, obedient instrument which he can apply to whatsoever end he thinks fit. Unless a popular leader appears, the army is Trotsky’s as long as he can feed it.
There are those who have long believed an internal military coup to be imminent, organized by old-time generals such as Brusilov, Baluev, Rattel, Gutov, Parsky, Klembovsky and others, whose names are associated with the highest military posts in Soviet Russia. Three things militate against the early success of such a coup. First, the experience of internal conspiracies shows it to be next to impossible to conspire against the Extraordinary Commission. Secondly, the memory of White administrations is still too fresh in the minds of the common soldier.

===Chinese===

倘若它们非政治化、由明白人主持，以纯然开启民智为唯一鹄的，原可办成教育与文化的真利器。如今它们常显得荒唐。但好歹代表一种"向上"的趋向，同布尔什维克的多数机构相比，堪称一股可喜的清流。

---

红军诸要项的考察至此已毕，可总结如下：

一、一部军事机器，具备其他军队的一切属性，所异者唯术语。1920年底其兵力号称约二百万，多半夸大。

二、一个附带的组织，规模约为全军的十分之一，即共产党组织，渗透全军：纯军事决断上听命于军事专家，政治与行政监督上则握绝对之权；辅之以非常委员会特设科、革命法庭和剿逃特别委员会。

三、一张由共产党人控制的宣传组织网，称文化启蒙委员会，以娱乐与教育士兵为务。

俄国人民尽管顺服、驯良、无首领，红军里建起的这部机器，仍是它的缔造者托洛茨基钢铁意志与无情决心的纪念碑。它发展迅猛，或许尚未完成。托洛茨基要把它造成一件绝对无魂、无志、唯命是从的工具，随心所欲，指向哪里用到哪里。只要没有深得民心的领袖出现，军队就是托洛茨基的——只要他还养得起它。

早就有人相信，一场内部军事政变一触即发，组织者是布鲁西洛夫、巴卢耶夫、拉特尔、古托夫、帕尔斯基、克伦博夫斯基等旧日将领——这些名字连着苏维埃俄国的最高军职。有三大障碍使这样的政变难望早成。其一，历次内部密谋的教训表明：密谋反对非常委员会，几乎不可能。其二，白色政权的记忆，在普通士兵心里还太新鲜。

===Original===

Thirdly, these generals suffer from the same defect as Wrangel, Denikin, and Kolchak, in that they are not politicians and have no concrete programme to offer the Russian people.
The local popularity of peasant leaders such as the “little fathers,” Balahovitch in Belorussia and Makhno in Ukrainia, who denounce Bolsheviks, Tsars, and landlords alike, shows that could a bigger man than these be found to fire the imagination of the peasantry on a nationwide scale the hoped-for national peasant uprising might become a reality. Until such a figure arises it is not to outside pressure or internal militarist conspiracies that we must look for the decay of Bolshevism, but must seek the signs of it in the very heart and core of the Communist Party. Such signs are already coming to light, and indicate sooner or later cataclysmic developments﻿—unless the decay is forestalled by what Pilsudski, the Socialist president of the Polish Republic, foresees as a possibility. Pilsudski spent many years in exile in Siberia for revolutionary agitation against the Tsar and knows Russia through and through. He foresees the possibility that the entire Russian population, maddened with hunger, disease, and despair, may eventually rise and sweep down on western Europe in a frantic quest for food and warmth.
Such a point will not be reached as long as the peasant, successfully defying Bolshevist administration, continues to produce sufficient for his own requirements. It needs, however, but some severe stress of nature, such as the droughts which periodically visit the country, to reduce the people to that condition. Will anything be able to stop such an avalanche? Should it ever begin, the once so ardently looked-for Russian steam roller will at last have become an awful, devastating reality.

===Chinese===

其三，这些将军患有同弗兰格尔、邓尼金和高尔察克一样的毛病：不是政客，拿不出向俄国人民交代的具体纲领。

农家领袖在地方上得人心——白俄罗斯的巴拉霍维奇、乌克兰的马赫诺这些"小父亲"，布尔什维克、沙皇、地主一体痛斥——可见只要能出一个比他们更雄大的人物，在全国范围点燃农民的想象，那众望所归的全民农民起义未必不能成真。在此人出现之前，布尔什维主义的衰亡，不可指望于外力压迫或军中密谋，而须到共产党的心脏与内核里去寻征兆。这样的征兆已经崭露，预示着迟早的剧变——除非这衰亡被抢先一步。波兰共和国的社会党总统毕苏斯基预见到一种可能。毕苏斯基因反沙皇的鼓动在西伯利亚流放多年，对俄国烂熟于心。他预见到这样的可能：全体俄国人口，被饥饿、疫病和绝望逼疯，最终起而横扫西欧，疯狂地觅食觅暖。

只要农民顶住布尔什维克当局，继续种出够自己吃的粮食，这一步就不会走到。然而只消来一场天时的重压——比如周期性降临的旱灾——便能教万民沦入那般田地。这样一场雪崩，还有什么挡得住？它一旦开始，那曾被如此热望的俄国压路机，就终于要变成一部骇人的、扫荡一切的现实了。

## X The Sphinx / 十 斯芬克斯
===Original===

A detailed narrative of my experiences during the following six months would surpass the dimensions to which I must limit this book. Some of them I hope to make the subject of a future story. For I met other “Stepanovnas,” “Marias” and “Journalists,” in whom I came to trust as implicitly as in the old and who were a very present help in time of trouble. I also inevitably met with scoundrels, but though No. 2 Gorokhovaya again got close upon my track﻿—even closer than through Zorinsky﻿—and one or two squeaks were very narrow indeed, still I have survived to tell the tale.
This is partly because the precautions I took to avoid detection became habitual. Only on one occasion was I obliged to destroy documents of value, while of the couriers who, at grave risk, carried communications back and forth from Finland, only two failed to arrive and, I presume, were caught and shot. But the messages they bore (as indeed any notes I ever made) were composed in such a manner that they could not possibly be traced to any individual or address.
I wrote mostly at night, in minute handwriting on tracing-paper, with a small india-rubber bag about four inches in length, weighted with lead, ready at my side. In case of alarm all my papers could be slipped into this bag and within thirty seconds be transferred to the bottom of a tub of washing or the cistern of the water-closet. In efforts to discover arms or incriminating documents, I have seen pictures, carpets, and bookshelves removed and everything turned topsy-turvy by diligent searchers, but it never occurred to anybody to search through a pail of washing or thrust his hand into the water-closet cistern.
Through the agency of friends I secured a post as draftsman at a small factory on the outskirts of the city. A relative of one of the officials of this place, whose signature was attached to my papers and who is well known to the Bolsheviks, called on me recently in New York. I showed him some notes I had made on the subject, but he protested that, camouflaged though my references were, they might still be traced to individuals concerned, most of whom, with their families, are still in Russia. I therefore suppressed them. For similar reasons I am still reticent in details concerning the regiment of the Red army to which I was finally attached.

===Chinese===

此后六个月经历的详尽叙述，将超出本书必须自限的篇幅。其中一些，我希望留作日后另一本书的题目。因为我还遇到了别的"斯捷潘诺夫娜"、"玛丽亚"和"记者"，我对他们的信任同对故人一样毫无保留，他们在患难时刻都是实实在在的援手。我也难免碰上恶棍；虽然戈罗霍瓦亚街2号又一次逼近了我的行迹——比通过佐林斯基那次还近——有一两回吱呀之声真是险之又险，我到底活下来讲这段故事了。

这一半是因为，我防人耳目的措施已成习惯。贵重文件我只销毁过一回；冒大险往返芬兰递送信件的信使，只有两名没能到达——我料想是被抓住枪毙了。但他们携带的情报（正如我历来的一切笔记）都写成那个样子：无论如何追不到任何个人或地址头上。

我多半夜里写，用蝇头小字写在高描图纸上，手边备着一只约四英寸长的橡皮小囊，坠着铅。一有警报，全部纸片即可滑进囊中，三十秒内转移到洗衣盆底或抽水马桶的水箱里。为了搜查武器或罪证文件，我见过搜查者搬走画、地毯和书架，把一切翻个底朝天；可从来没人想过要翻一盆洗衣裳，或把手伸进马桶水箱。

经朋友们张罗，我在城郊一家小工厂谋得一个绘图员的差事。这家工厂一名官员的亲戚——他的签名就附在我的证件上，布尔什维克方面颇有名气——前不久在纽约来看我。我给他看了我就此事写的几页笔记，他却竭力抗议：纵然我的指涉都做了伪装，仍可能被人追索到相关的人——他们大多还连同家眷在俄国。我便删去了。出于同样的缘故，对我最终编入的那支红军团队，我在细节上也仍守口如瓶。

===Original===

Learning through military channels at my disposal that men of my age and industrial status were shortly to be mobilized and despatched to the eastern front, where the advance of Kolchak was growing to be a serious menace, I forestalled the mobilization order by about a week and applied for admission as a volunteer in the regiment of an officer acquaintance, stationed a short distance outside Petrograd. There was some not unnatural hesitation before I received an answer, due to the necessity of considering the personality of the regimental commissar﻿—a strong Communist who wished to have the regiment despatched to perform its revolutionary duty against Kolchak’s armies. But at the critical moment this individual was promoted to a higher divisional post, and the commander succeeded in getting nominated to his regiment a commissar of shaky communistic principles, who ultimately developed anti-Bolshevist sympathies almost as strong as his own. How my commander, a Tsarist officer, who detested and feared the Communists, was forced to serve in the Red army I will explain later.
Despite his ill-concealed sympathies, however, this gentleman won Trotsky’s favour in an unexpected and remarkable manner. Being instructed to impede an advance of the forces of the “White” general, Yudenich, by the destruction of strategic bridges, he resolved to blow up the wrong bridge, and, if possible, cut off the Red retreat and assist the White advance. By sheer mistake, however, the company he despatched to perform the task blew up the right bridge, thus covering a precipitate Red retreat and effectually checking the White advance.
For days my commander secretly tore his hair and wept, his mortification rendered the more acute by the commendations he was obliged for the sake of appearances to shower upon his men, whose judgment had apparently been so superior to his own. His chagrin reached its height when he received an official communication from army headquarters applauding the timely exploit, while through the Communist organization he was formally invited to join the privileged ranks of the Communist Party! In the view of my commander no affront could have been more offensive than this unsought Bolshevist honour.

===Chinese===

我通过手头的军方渠道获悉：我这种年龄和工籍的人不久将被动员，派往东部战线——高尔察克的推进正日益成为严重的威胁。我便抢在动员令前约莫一个星期，申请自愿入伍，投奔一位军官相识的团，驻地离彼得格勒不远。接到答复前有过一阵不算意外的踌躇：得先掂量团政委的为人——一个强硬的共产党人，一心想叫全团开拔去对高尔察克的军队尽"革命义务"。可正在节骨眼上，此君高升到师部去了，团长设法给本团弄来一位共产党原则摇摇晃晃的政委，此人后来发展出的反布尔什维克同情，几乎同团长本人一样强烈。我的团长——一位憎恶并惧怕共产党人的沙皇军官——如何被迫进了红军，容我稍后再讲。

然而，尽管同情心藏都藏不住，这位先生却以一个出人意料而了不起的方式赢得了托洛茨基的青睐。他奉命破坏战略性桥梁，以阻遏"白"军将军尤登尼奇的推进；他却决心炸错桥，并且可能的话，截断红军退路，助白军一臂之力。谁知阴差阳错，他派去执行任务的那支工兵偏偏炸对了桥，结果掩护了红军一场仓皇的撤退，还牢牢挡住了白军的攻势。

一连几天，我的团长偷偷扯头发、抹眼泪；而他为了场面不得不对部下大加褒奖——夸他们眼力显然高过自己——这份憋屈就更加锥心。当集团军总部发来公函，为这一"及时"的功绩击节称赏，且他经共产党组织被正式邀请加入享有特权的共产党时，他的懊恼达到了顶点。在我的团长看来，再没有比这份不请自来的布尔什维克荣恩更难堪的羞辱了。

===Original===

He was utterly at a loss to grasp my point of view when I told him what to me was obvious, namely, that no offer could have been more providential and that he ought to jump at it. Though inside Russia the approaching White armies were often imagined to be a band of noble and chivalrous crusaders, certain information I had received as to the disorganization prevailing amongst them aroused my misgivings, and I was very doubtful whether my commander’s error had materially altered the course of events. The commissar, who did not care one way or the other, saw the humour of the situation. He, too, urged the commander to smother his feelings and see the joke, with the result that the would-be traitor to the pseudo-proletarian cause became a Communist, and combining his persuasions with those of the commissar, succeeded in keeping the regiment out of further action for several weeks. The confidence they had won made it easy to convince army headquarters that the regiment was urgently required to suppress uprisings which were feared in the capital. When disturbances did break out, however, the quelling of them was entrusted to troops drafted from the far south or east, for it was well known that no troops indigenous to Petrograd or Moscow could be relied upon to fire on their fellow-townspeople.
I had hitherto evaded military service as long as possible, fearing that it might impede the conduct of my intelligence work. The contrary proved to be the case, and for many reasons I regretted I had not enlisted earlier. Apart from greater freedom of movement and preference over civilians when applying for lodging, amusement, or travelling tickets, the Red soldier received rations greatly superior both in quantity and quality to those of the civilian population. Previous to this time I had received only half a pound of bread daily and had had to take my scanty dinner at a filthy communal eating-house, but as a Red soldier I received, besides a dinner and other odds and ends not worth mentioning, a pound and sometimes a pound and a half of tolerably good black bread, which alone was sufficient, accustomed as I am to a scanty diet, to subsist on with relative comfort.
The commander was a good fellow, nervous and sadly out of place in “the party,” but he soon got used to it and enjoyed its many privileges. He stood me in good stead.

===Chinese===

当我告诉他，在我看来再没有比这更天赐良机的提议、他应当一把抓住时，他全然无法理解我的立场。在俄国国内，人们常把逼近的白军想象成一队高贵的、骑士风度的十字军；而我收到的某些关于其内部紊乱的情报令我疑虑丛生，我很怀疑团长的失误是否真左右了大局。那位政委倒是不在乎白红哪头，一眼看出局面里的滑稽。他也劝团长把感情捂起来，笑纳这桩趣事；结果，这位伪无产阶级事业的准叛徒成了共产党人，又同政委你一言我一语，成功地把全团在好几个星期里调离了战事。他们挣来的信用，使集团军总部轻而易举地相信：京城里怕是要出乱子，本团亟须留下弹压。可乱子真起来时，弹压的任务却交给了从遥远的南方或东方调来的部队——因为谁都清楚，彼得格勒或莫斯科土生土长的部队，绝靠不住朝自己的乡亲开枪。

在此之前我一直尽量躲避兵役，唯恐妨碍情报工作。结果恰恰相反，好几个方面都叫我后悔没早点入伍。除了行动更自由、在申请住宿、娱乐或车票时优先于平民之外，红军士兵的口粮，无论数量质量都大大优于平民。此前我每天只得半磅面包，还得在一家肮脏的公共食堂用那顿寒酸的午饭；当了红军士兵，除一顿午饭和些不值一提的零碎，我能领一磅、有时一磅半尚可的黑面包——单凭这个，像我这样惯于清苦的人，就足以活得相当舒坦。

团长是个好人，神经质，在"党内"很不自在，但他很快就惯了，还把种种特权享受了个够。他帮了我大忙。

===Original===

Repeatedly detailing me off to any place I wished to go to, on missions he knew were lengthy (such as the purchase of automobile tyres, which were unobtainable, or literature of various kinds), I was able to devote my main attention as before to the political and economic situation.
As a Red soldier, I was sent to Moscow and there consulted with the National Centre, the most promising of the political organizations whose object was to work out a programme acceptable to the Russian people as a whole. On account of its democratic character this organization was pursued by the Bolshevist Government with peculiar zeal, and was finally unearthed, and its members, of whom many were Socialists, shot.3 From Moscow also I received regularly copies of the summaries on the general situation that were submitted to the Soviet of People’s Commissars. The questions I was instructed in messages from abroad to investigate covered the entire field of soviet administration, but I do not propose to deal with that huge subject here. It is the present and the inscrutable future that fascinate me rather than the past. I will speak only of the peasantry, the army, and “the party.” For it is on the ability or inability of the Communists to control the army that the stability of the Bolshevist regime in a considerable measure depends, while the future lies in the lap of that vast inarticulate mass of simple peasant toilers, so justly termed the Russian Sphinx.

===Chinese===

他一再差遣我到我想去的任何地方，去办那些他知道拖得长的差事（比如采购根本买不到的汽车轮胎，或形形色色的书刊），于是我得以同先前一样，把主要精力放在政治和经济局势上。

身为红军士兵，我被派往莫斯科，在那里同"国家中心"有过商议——在以拟订一个全体俄国人民都能接受的纲领为宗旨的政治组织中，它是最有希望的一个。正因其民主性质，布尔什维克政府对它的追剿格外起劲，最终将其破获，成员多人——其中不少是社会主义者——被枪决。3在莫斯科，我还定期收到呈送人民委员苏维埃的总体局势摘要的副本。国外来信指示我调查的问题，覆盖苏维埃行政的全域；但这个大题目我不打算在此展开。令我着迷的是现在和不可测的未来，而非过去。我只谈农民、军队和"党"。因为布尔什维克政权能否稳住，相当程度上系于共产党人控制军队的能力；而未来，则躺在那庞大而无声的朴实农民劳动者群体的膝上——他们被如此恰当地称作俄国的斯芬克斯。
